\subsection{A core-sparse Monge reduction with terminal penalties}
\paragraph{Independent evidence.}
The condensed matrix harness passed 1,600 random rectangular products,
checking 251,779 output values and exact leftmost witnesses by enumeration,
and 1,600 arbitrary submatrix selections. Sparse distance-matrix products
at dimensions 128, 512, and 2048 each retain seven nonzero output core
entries from seven entries in each input. Core visits remain 673 in these
three checks, while dimension scans grow. This exercises nontrivial sparse
output, not just products with empty cores.

The complete Python constructor passed 1,134 solves, including 682 exhaustive
binary-weight cases through four keys (including two empty-key inputs),
838 cubic checks, and 32 fixed-budget controls that disable early stopping.
On 78 layers of the small controls, all 3,367 interval values and leftmost
witnesses agree with explicit all-root enumeration. Full tree audits check
15,544 reconstructed keys. Geometric and folded weights through 64 keys
remain adversarial controls; the geometric input requires all 64 layers.

The C++ address/undefined-behavior sanitizer run passed 1,278 complete solves,
including 826 independent cubic checks, 12 Python compact comparisons,
random weights through 48 keys, geometric/folded inputs through 64 keys,
and all-zero and single-active-gap controls. It checked 18,248 reconstructed
keys. The release run passed 1,362 solves and 88,904 reconstructed keys,
including 1,302 independent Python Knuth comparisons. All release cases
also compare against the separately implemented C++ Knuth constructor.
The independent parent-array audit verifies inorder validity, unique key
coverage, and direct mixed weighted cost. Three malformed/out-of-domain
inputs are explicitly rejected by each binary. No sanitizer error was
reported. Results include source hashes, compiler flags, and seeds.

The dense falsification harness \texttt{centered\_}\allowbreak\texttt{layers.py} passed 1,100
complete solves, 824 cubic comparisons, 3,317 all-root cells, 1,538 baseline
all-shape checks, 99,087 baseline-density checks, 685,288 tile checks, and
2,327 scale checks. It explicitly computed 4,153,053 dense states and
8,223,340 root candidates and reconstructed 11,656 keys. Baselines include

At 256 keys, three large-baseline fixtures have deviations 501, 505, and 504,

are budget comparisons, not measured speedups: the original constructor

The actual condensed variant \texttt{centered\_obst.py} passed 999 complete
solves, 790 cubic comparisons, 32 fixed-budget controls, 1,001 intermediate

The dense \texttt{ruler\_layers.py} diagnostic checked 921 layer solves,

\subsection{Global alternating-gap bounds and linear profile construction}
\paragraph{Independent evidence and full-construction counts}\label{sec:legacy-24-4}
The exact word/tree harness preceded the production constructor. Recognition

13,834 parity/tree inequalities and 69,170 mixed-weight/tree inequalities
through nine keys, including exact equality of each size/parity minimum
with the gap formula. The perfect-size counterexample is retained.

The full suite passes 3,157 standalone solves against Knuth, including

unrestricted shape DP. Another 3,157 routed solves agree. There are 1,201

The remaining 1,956 route to exact fallback: 86 have unsupported key

Zeros, ties, huge integers, random, geometric, near-uniform, arbitrary

are included.

Both scaling families now have no added baseline. At 393,216 keys,
a nonperiodic witness-generated pattern has 262,111 joint-signature runs
but only 196,305 interval transitions; strict alternation has 393,216 runs
and 196,608 transitions. They require 212,793 and 262,144 membership

Each orders two multiplicity records, expands 393,216 key weights,

uses a residual-Huffman contraction or a dense shape state. These are
operation categories, not machine-instruction totals or measured peak bytes.
Ten scaling portfolio controls agree; the two 1536-key cases also match
Knuth and the unrestricted shape DP. Larger cases use the proved global
bound and direct output-cost verification.

The threshold probe preceded the production constructor. Across all

At $n=327679$, there are $327679$ distinct key weights and $65538$

\subsection{Shifted alphabetic certificates beyond key-capacity equality}

The independent probe precedes the optimized constructor: $960$ cubic

It certifies $138$ candidates, including $24$ strictly above the old bound.
The constructor checks all $9840$ ternary gap vectors through seven keys
against cubic values and the literal compatible-pair implementation,

Random, near-uniform, geometric, binary, and zero inputs are included.
Eight family sizes through $895$ keys match Knuth; the four smallest also

Five large constructions reach $n=229375$, with $196608$ distinct key

\subsection{Endpoint prices and the limit of additive transfers}
\paragraph{Independent evidence and measured costs}\label{sec:legacy-28-4}
The independent harness precedes the optimizer. It checks $2052$ literal
transfer identities and exact cost-difference ranks through eight keys:
rank $2n-2$, nullity three. For $60$ three- and four-key instances, an
independent arrangement of all alphabetic-tree and key-rank breakpoints
evaluates $5111$ rational points; every maximum agrees with the price search.
On $600$ mixed cases through $64$ keys, $1712$ individual oracle values
match independent cubic values, and small cases also match literal trees.
All $600$ bounded full solves match cubic, Knuth, and direct cost: $220$
certify, including $152$ previous rejections, and $380$ fall back.
The uncapped diagnostic certifies $228$, rescues $160$, and completes
every maximum within ten calls in this suite. There are $358$ strict
bound gaps and $600$ clamped-transfer monotonicity controls.

Nineteen exact reduction controls produce the four-key obstruction.
A further $6000$ seeded positive cases find no nonintegral price maximum;
this is not an integrality theorem. Intermediate prices are fractional:
the obstruction search queries $y=1/3$ and obtains $F=68/3$. The code
uses exact rationals and does not assume integral maxima.




\subsection{Root/nonroot coupling beyond the transfer barrier}
\paragraph{Independent controls, regressions, and a retained conjecture}\label{sec:legacy-29-2}
The independent case-partition harness precedes the optimized constructor.
Across 840 mixed instances through eight keys, it checks 246480 literal
conditioned inequalities. The bound strictly improves $K+A$ on 373 cases
and is exact on 324. Random, near-uniform, geometric, and zero/binary inputs
are included.

The normalized full implementation matches cubic, Knuth, and reconstructed
original cost on 1040 instances through 64 keys; 720 also match literal tree
enumeration. There are 800 independent bound controls and six additional
family checks. The route certifies 505, including all 240 constant-size base
cases, and falls back on 535. It exceeds the completed maximum transfer bound
in 84 cases and rescues 11 price rejections, but loses 47 price certificates.
These finite counts are not distributional guarantees, and the fixed endpoint
choice is not universally superior to price optimization.



A separate diagnostic retains an unproved marked-boundary event family.
It inserts a barrier at one weighted interior key, permits ordinary
compatible merges on either side, and charges the key weight when a pair
first consumes that barrier. Greedily taking the cheapest event fails:
for $p=(0,4,0)$, $q=(8,10,2,18)$, it returns 82 instead of 80, with strict
primary choices. Inverting a unit uniform transfer gives the positive
instance $p=(1,5,1)$, $q=(7,9,1,17)$, with costs 79 and 77 respectively.

The alternative probe tries the cheapest crossing pair after every prefix
of ordinary merges, completes compatible merging, and evaluates reconstructed
trees with the original positional key cost. It matches all 17769 checked
instances, including 15769 literal-tree controls; 4134 controls also enumerate
every crossing choice without changing the best result. This is a conjectural
candidate family, not an exact solver theorem. There are 20800 invalid depth
reconstructions across the attempts, which are rejected. Merge charges are
not assumed to equal positional costs.

Its candidate costs are not unimodal in the activation prefix. With gaps
$(17,83,93,11)$ and weight 92 on key 1, the ordinary merges cost 100 and 104;
activation before, between, or after them gives costs 500,681,500. Thus a
single-valley search is unjustified even with positive gaps and strict
ordinary priorities. The full probe records 6759 nonunimodal sequences,
116 greedy failures, and 65 charge/cost disagreements. No subquadratic
complexity is claimed for the probe. The proved root/nonroot certificate
does not depend on this conjecture.


\subsection{Exact checks and preliminary measurements}
\label{sec:legacy-14}

The code uses nonnegative integer weights, an independent cubic recurrence,
Knuth's recurrence, and direct evaluation of reconstructed trees.
The basic harness additionally enumerates all tree shapes for small sizes.
CPU experiments ran on the sponsor's M4 Mac mini. The reported quantities
are algorithmic counts, not inferred asymptotic bounds from elapsed time.

\begin{itemize}
\item Single-key contraction: 22,143 exhaustive weight assignments in
$\{0,1,2\}$ through $n=4$, and 6,000 seeded random instances through $n=20$.
Every cost and reconstructed tree matched the cubic oracle.
\item Block theorem: 19,486 qualifying interval contractions across 4,500
seeded instances through $n=16$ matched independent cubic costs.
\item Block algorithm: 3,000 random instances through $n=20$, including
fixed and adaptive thresholds, matched the cubic oracle and tree evaluation.
Additional small cases check threshold selection against every threshold.
\end{itemize}

\label{tab:states}

In the heavy-separator instance all gaps and ordinary keys have integer
weights in $\{1,2,3\}$, and 22 randomly selected keys have weight $10^6$.
The block algorithm retains exactly 22 keys. Its state reduction is about
12.7-fold, but this is not a claim of a 12.7-fold runtime speedup.
On ordinary random mixed weights the gain is small.
The single-key pass contracts every key for the tested sequence
$p_i=4^i,q_i=4^i$, but not for most of the ratio-two sequence.
Geometric integer inputs also illustrate why bit complexity must be
distinguished from arithmetic-operation complexity.


\subsection{Additional shape and contraction controls}
The recursive constructor passed 3,000 random mixed inputs through thirty
keys against cubic recurrence and direct tree evaluation. The minimum-path-
length optimizer matched literal shape enumeration on 360 weighted inputs
through eight keys. Its full fallback variants matched 3,000 further inputs.
The affine-corridor audit includes independent candidate, regret, and complete
construction checks; its parameter-dependent state bound is proved in the main
text. Source-hashed records retain the individual operation categories.

\subsection{Additional recurrence and certificate controls}
\paragraph{Reference checks and implementation boundary.}
The dense audit \texttt{penalty\_layers.py} passed 1,092 complete solves:
680 exhaustive binary-weight inputs through four keys, 384 random inputs
through 32 keys, and 28 size/family fixtures through 256 keys. These include
zeros, near-uniform, alternating, varying mixed, and geometric weights.
All complete values and trees match Knuth and direct cost evaluation.
There are 7,972 independent cubic interval checks, 8,048 all-root cell
checks, 792 explicit padded-product checks, 792 all-shape truncated-layer
checks, and 139,992 verified dyadic tiles. The geometric family correctly
needs all 64 layers at 64 keys; no logarithmic-in-$n$ claim is made for
its exponentially large mass.

At 256 keys, the maximum interior core over all layers was 3,857 for
mixed weights in $\{1,2\}$, 6,196 for weights in $\{100,101,102\}$,
and 5,008 for mixed weights in $\{1,\ldots,16\}$, against 32,640 possible
interior positions. These are representation counts, not runtime speedups.
The audit actually computed 6,318,862 dense states and examined 12,381,033
root candidates across its full suite. It constructed 11,912 keys.
Source-hashed raw data is in \texttt{penalty\_layers.json}.

The lexicographic oracle matched 400 exhaustive shape-profile comparisons
through eight keys. Uncapped search matched exhaustive evaluation of all
allowed integer prices on 1,200 inputs. On 3,000 additional full-OBST
inputs through thirty keys, both capped fallback versions matched cubic
DP and direct tree costs; 87 previously unsuccessful unrestricted
certificates were recovered. A separate seeded sweep recovered the
$n=1,024$, amplitude 2 input with two additional oracle calls, and the
$n=2,048$, amplitude 1 input with three. Larger tested perturbations
usually exceeded the profile ceiling. This applies standard concave-price
optimization to the certificate; it is not a general subquadratic result.

In the seeded 2,048-key case, the candidate costs 43,032,446; the bound
rises from 43,031,347 at $\delta=0$ to 43,032,459 at $\delta=125$.
The three extra passes use 6,144 contractions and 6,141 pair comparisons,
in addition to 4,096 shape states, 6,142 transitions, 20,784 sorting
comparisons, and 2,752 original merge comparisons. Knuth uses 2,098,176
interval states and 4,198,710 root candidates on that input.

Twelve full constructions reach $n=262143$, with 262141 distinct key weights,
262144 distinct gap weights, and cost 92530765201355. The best preceding
transfer bound is 91912294498251, a deficit of 618470703104. The largest run
uses three alphabetic calls, 524285 compatible merges, 13894100 priority
comparisons, 17365446 heap-item comparisons, and 1482337 key-sort comparisons.
All scans, heap events, rebasing, and output visits are recorded; there are
no interval-DP cells. Seven sizes through 511 keys match Knuth and three
match cubic. Larger sizes match a separately constructed perfect tree and
the closed form. Six smaller sizes additionally complete the exact endpoint
price search and verify the full-class deficit independently.

Both oracles passed 50 comparisons against exhaustive length-sequence
minimization, 5,000 arbitrary-multiplier bound checks against cubic OBST,
and 1,050 direct comparisons of the full and shortened oracles.
Forced-root marginals were additionally checked against exact
root-constrained costs.

The initial probe checks $1092$ alphabetic controls, $2400$ mixed controls,
$616500$ literal tree evaluations, and $42563$ pair evaluations: $2385$
candidates are exact and $15$ are suboptimal. Exhausting key multisets
and ordered gaps over $\{0,1\}$ and $\{1,2\}$ through seven keys gives
$7172$ cases, including $14$ binary failures and no $\{1,2\}$ failures;
the latter finite observation is not a theorem. There are $2036$
constant-key controls, $6918$ profile/depth identities, and $1421$
transfer controls. All $8192$ integer corners of $1000(p,q)\pm1$ retain
a gap at least $996$. Padding is checked by Knuth through $256$ keys
(largest weight $732$ bits), with cubic checks through $16$.

All $512$ integer corners of
$100(p,q)\pm1$ are checked; the smallest observed gap is $94$, above
the proved lower bound $56$. With all nine weights distinct, the example
\[
 p=(996,997,1998,999),\qquad q=(1000,2001,1002,1003,1004)
\]
has full optimum $23999$ and maximal transfer bound $23004$. A constant
supporting plane certifies that maximum in one call.

\subsection{Coarse optimal families and complete fine-weight refinement}
\label{app:coarse-face}
The independent audit checks 716 coarse inputs: all 680 binary mixed
inputs through four keys, and 36 seeded inputs at $n=5,8,12$.
All 7540 interval minimum/maximum optimal-root pairs agree with a separate
all-root recurrence. Independent optimal-tree counts agree with every
uniqueness decision; 311 coarse optima are unique. The audit then checks
1432 complete signed-rational refinements against the original all-root
optimum, original-weight tree traversal, and coarse optimum. Every fine
payload cell count equals $\sum_jm_j(m_j+1)/2$.

Six further controls exercise automatic scale selection; four exercise
certificate rejection. Three zero-coarse-mass controls preserve the full
optimal family, including optima taller than a balanced sufficient height.
Twenty queries reuse four prepared partitions, agree with independent
all-root optima, and perform no new Monge products. The record
\texttt{coarse\_face\_audit.json} retains its seed, counts and eight
executed-source hashes.

The scale audit constructs eight unstructured lattice perturbations.
Coarse weights are independently drawn from $\{0,\ldots,7\}$; a large
integer scale and distinct position perturbations make all $2n+1$ original
weights different. Automatic scale selection accepts at $m=7$ in each
retained row. Complete construction includes both coarse orientations,
the unrestricted-child closure products, partition extraction, and every
fine payload. Selected exact counts are:

Fine DP cells and core visits are separate operation categories. Fewer

No measured exponent or distributional bound on payload sizes is claimed.

Four ordinary mixed inputs at the same sizes reject all sixteen candidate
scales and use Knuth. Three non-perfect uniform coarse inputs at
$n=32,64,128$ retain the entire payload table, as predicted by the explicit
root-ambiguity example. Twelve further full queries reuse four perfect-size
uniform preparations at $n=31,63,127,255$ and require neither fine DP cells
nor new products. These uniform bases already admit direct linear construction.
All 27 outputs agree with independent unrestricted Knuth and a separate
original-weight tree traversal. The record \texttt{coarse\_face\_scale.json}
retains every input, separate preparation and reference counters, all failed
routes, exact accepted scales and error magnitudes, seed and eight source
hashes. Bit-operation costs are separate from these arithmetic counts.

\label{app:coarse-structure}
The harness first compares the entire reachable interval and root-triple
sets with literal optimal-tree enumeration on forty inputs through eight
keys. It evaluates $10{,}275$ literal tree costs. Twenty-seven separate
perturbations select each uniform coarse optimum uniquely, checked against
every competing small tree. Four exact scales verify the five-key strict
rotation trap, its full optimum and all four neighboring costs. The
three-key root-hole example is checked by an independent all-root recurrence.

For $B=2,4,8,16,32$, a separate all-root computation builds the complete
coarse-optimal grammar. Every interval in
\eqref{eq:coarse-grammar-intervals} is checked for reachability and for its
entire optimal-root set. All $1{,}493$ selected intervals also receive an
explicit full-tree witness, whose original coarse cost equals the
independent optimum. Selected counts are:

For each size, a shuffled permutation of $1,\ldots,2n+1$ gives distinct

a direct full-tree cost traversal agree on all five fine inputs; each
returned tree is also coarse-optimal. These are complete original-weight
controls, not a new fast constructor.

The direct reference computations use $24{,}972$ interval states and

are verification operations. Larger rows through $B=2^{20}$ evaluate the
proved formulas only, with no graph enumeration. The record
\texttt{coarse\_face\_structure.json} retains the seed, all five original
fine inputs, full costs, reference counts, exact scale controls and both
executed-source hashes.

The independent harness establishes the two-tree family before importing

For 96 coarse inputs through twelve keys, including all-zero inputs,

serves three independently perturbed rational fine inputs: all 288 full

Ninety-six zero-budget preparations verify exact fallback, and the
three-key root-hole fixture verifies that the missing middle root is

The direct reference computations total $20{,}900$ states and
$224{,}951$ root candidates.

The two-tree scaling rows run complete generic preparation through

Three unstructured integer bases at $n=32,64,128$ have respectively

For the nine-key example in Section~\ref{sec:coarse-structure}, the
28 minimum-IPL shapes are checked against all $4{,}862$ full shapes.
The fourteen distinct coarse-preserving neighbors, combining every

224,226,227,228,229,230,230,230,231,231,234,235,238,239.

The selected local tree has error cost $219$, and the minimum among

Every descendant promotion, including those leaving the coarse family,

the retained grammar directly.

The harness first checks the signed allowance using independent all-root

checks the per-depth $2K$ density charge, product-core bound, index-node

The complete constructor audit checks 108 original-weight instances,

\subsection{Implicit ordered references and nonlinear corridor audit}
\label{app:ordered-reference}
The interval-oracle harness establishes its claims with independent all-root

including both orientations and partial valid-root ranges. Each threshold
query also satisfies its binary-search call bound.

Full refinement checks 144 arbitrary original-weight queries, 54 literal

fail both ordered exchange conditions, yet complete exact screening.
For example, at $n=767$ the coarse index stores 7,670 dyadic entries; the
sparse-bump query reaches 959 intervals and 1,067 roots and makes 3,482
root-cost calls. The ordinary and densely perturbed failure cases retain

The nonlinear-corridor harness separately constructs literal expansion
words before importing its solver. It verifies 1,638 exchange-regret
inequalities and 530 optimal-prefix inclusions. The implemented class
optimizer then matches 128 literal class optima and their state bounds.
Full original construction passes 108 independent cubic controls, 84 literal
full-tree controls, eighteen rational controls and twenty direct uniform
controls. Forty-two scaling controls through $n=1{,}535$ match independent
Knuth: twenty-eight curved, irregular and reflected bounded-error cases
satisfy the nonlinear-family guarantee; ordinary random and geometric cases

The larger curved instances use

A separate dense diagnostic covers eight residual patterns at seventeen

\subsection{Range certificates and sharp box-threshold audit}
\label{app:shape-range}
The independent harness was written before the range-based constructor.
It enumerates 6,917 trees through nine keys to check both shortening-capacity
bounds, including 435 perfect-size gap charges. It checks every joint
key/gap box vertex at three and seven keys, as well as separate key-only
and gap-only boxes: 33,304 exact all-root comparisons at the stated threshold.
The explicit shifted-perfect construction is checked at fifteen sizes
through $n=65{,}535$, for three key/gap range pairs and below, at and above
the threshold, giving 135 exact cost-difference identities. Fifty-four
small boundary cases also have independent Knuth controls and test the full
constructor's direct or fallback decision.

Full construction is compared with 576 independent cubic optima, including
96 rational cases checked by exact scaling to integer reference inputs,
and 144 literal full-tree controls. Ninety-six fixtures separately satisfy
the general range guarantee. Twenty-eight larger inputs through $n=1{,}535$
are compared with independent Knuth. Fourteen curved or reflected inputs
are certified by the new range condition although all fail the earlier
$(n-1)E_0$ baseline gate. Ordinary random and geometric inputs retain
all rejection work before exact fallback.

The larger curved family uses
\[
 f_i=i+\lfloor i^2/n\rfloor,\qquad B=n(f_{n-1}+4)+2,
\]
with key errors $-1$ at multiples of three and $+1$ elsewhere, and gap
errors $-1$ at odd indices and $+1$ at even indices. Three full constructions
at $n=6{,}143$, $24{,}575$ and $98{,}303$ are checked by the proved range
certificate and direct original-weight traversal. These large rows have
no quadratic-reference comparison. The corridor states and transitions
are unchanged by subtracting the common baseline from the earlier curved
family; all fitting, certificate and output counts are recorded separately.
The certificate proof, rather than the measured state counts, supplies
Corollary~\ref{cor:range-nonlinear}. The completed record retains exact
inputs or formulas and eight executed-source hashes.

\section{Adversarial core search and the odd-ramp proof audit}
\label{app:core-search}
The search fixes nonnegative integer total mass at $W=n$ and maximizes
\[
 \frac{\max_{0\le h\le H}\delta(E_h)}{n\sqrt{W\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil}}.
\]
The core includes the $n$ finite-completion diagonal entries. All layer
costs and density counts use exact integers. A whole-table fixed point,
when reached, justifies repeating the same count for subsequent layers.
Single-unit and larger mass transfers, swaps, and reversals preserve the
mass constraint. Simulated annealing affects only search acceptance;
floating point is not used to compute costs or cores.

There are 48 starts over twelve sizes from 16 through 1,024, including
non-power-of-two sizes, and 24 additional concentrated starts at six sizes.
They perform 145,155 candidate evaluations in total. The first batch uses
spread mixed weights, key-only weights, and uniform initial keys; the second
uses spaced heavy keys, spaced ramps, clustered equal weights, and random
sparse positions. Each batch first checks 232 complete layer vectors against
the independent recurrence, with all-root and literal-tree checks on small
inputs; selected winners receive further comparisons. Search budgets, seeds,
full winning vectors, all layer counts, and operation counters are recorded.
The table reports the best result at each displayed size across these starts.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrr}
\toprule
$n=W$ & Largest completed core & Normalized ratio\\
\midrule
16 & 69 & 0.482152\\
32 & 185 & 0.417226\\
64 & 463 & 0.341792\\
128 & 1162 & 0.283691\\
256 & 2724 & 0.221680\\
512 & 6470 & 0.176604\\
1024 & 14582 & 0.134175\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

A separate diagnostic evaluates eight explicit patterns at fifteen sizes,
for 120 complete layer vectors through 4,096 keys. These include a contiguous
ramp, a contiguous random block, a spaced ramp, isolated key and gap mass,
binary blocks, ruler-weight sampling, and random unit placements. The
contiguous ramp gives 13,461, 36,276, and 98,168 completed core entries at
1,024, 2,048, and 4,096 keys, respectively. These measurements motivated the
odd-ramp family with exact mass $W=n$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:ramp-core-lower}.
The proof uses the separately specified odd weights, not a fitted formula
for the diagnostic's initial ramp.

The decreasing ratios in the first table alone would have been misleading
as evidence for a universal near-linear bound. The actual conclusion follows
from the theorem's explicit density coordinates. Search supplies candidate
patterns and opportunities for falsification; it supplies neither an upper
bound nor an asymptotic tightness certificate.

\paragraph{Independent proof controls.}
The new mathematical audit checks 2,652 straight and 2,652 bent rotation
identities, 894 dyadic partitions through $n=129$, and 4,277 scale bounds.
The odd-ramp audit compares its subsequence recurrence with 445 literal
subset enumerations and checks 388 perfect-slot embeddings against an
all-root interval recurrence. There are 42,748 independent checks of the
rightmost-zero formula and 17,688 checks of the closed-form density against
subsequence DP. At $n=1024$, all 1,896 cells in the proved set also match
the original penalty-layer recurrence. That original-layer audit and the
smaller controls evaluate 4,736,120 dense states and 8,852,066 root candidates.
They are diagnostics, not operations of the sparse construction algorithm.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrll}
\toprule
$n=W$ & Proved positive cells & Subsequence DP & Original-layer DP\\
\midrule
1024 & 1896 & checked & checked\\
4096 & 15792 & checked & ---\\
16384 & 128736 & formula only & ---\\
65536 & 1039296 & formula only & ---\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The last two rows evaluate the proved coordinate count and densities; they
are not additional independent full-layer computations. The lower bound
holds for every $n=2^{2t}$, $t\ge5$, by its proof. These records are in
\texttt{core\_size\_search*.json}, \texttt{core\_size\_patterns.json},
\texttt{core\_size\_lemmas.json}, and \texttt{ramp\_core\_tightness.json}.
Each records hashes of the exact sources used.

\section{Local tile charging: probe and exact one-key audit}
\label{app:local-tile}
The tile-mass probe evaluates mixed random, sparse, uniform, geometric,
and concentrated inputs, followed by fixed-mass local mutations. Its
objective is the ratio of a maximal tile's density mass to the mass of
its own span. The 1199 evaluations include 123 independent all-root
controls. They examine 19,699,184 dense states, 38,539,843 root candidates,
and 1,169,332 tiles. The largest observed ratio is four at $n=256$,
$h=7$, with one key carrying all the weight. Scaling that weight to one
leads to Theorem~\ref{thm:local-tile-obstruction}; the search alone does
not establish the unbounded ratio.

The separate formula audit checks every interval value and every interior
density of the explicit family through $n=1024$. It uses all-root
recurrences through $n=64$ and monotone-root recurrences at larger sizes.
There are 700,024 interval-formula checks, 697,992 density-formula checks,
and 1023 penalty-line checks. The exact completed core sizes are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrrrr}
\toprule
$n$ & $h$ & Completed core & Tile support & Tile mass\\
\midrule
16 & 3 & 33 & 0 & 0\\
32 & 4 & 71 & 1 & 1\\
64 & 5 & 149 & 2 & 2\\
128 & 6 & 307 & 3 & 3\\
256 & 7 & 625 & 4 & 4\\
512 & 8 & 1263 & 5 & 5\\
1024 & 9 & 2541 & 6 & 6\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The general one-key corollary is additionally checked on 1316 cases:
every key position through $n=16$, two positive masses, and all tested
layers, using literal all-root recurrence. These contribute 88,170
additional density checks. Together the formula harness records
6,387,678 dense diagnostic states and 12,975,174 root candidates.
Larger rows through $h=30$ evaluate only the proved formulas and are
explicitly labeled as such. They are not independent dense solves.

The executed sources and exact operation records are retained in
\texttt{dyadic\_mass\_probe.json} and \texttt{single\_key\_tile.json}.
The proofs in Section~\ref{sec:local-tile-obstruction} establish the
asymptotic local obstruction and global linear bound independently of
these finite checks.

\section{Independent mixed-slot construction audit}
\label{app:mixed-slots}
The reconstruction procedure is checked on all 32,902 nonempty slot subsets
of perfect trees through height four. Every surviving key has comparison
length no larger than its selected slot's length, and every gap satisfies
the neighboring-key identity.

The constructor has 717 independently checked complete solves, including
473 literal slot-minimum and literal tree-minimum controls. Its independent
height recurrence examines 558,638 interval states and 15,547,880 root
candidates, including the smaller scale controls. The positive four-key
example in Section~\ref{sec:mixed-slots} has full optimum $121$; literal
minimum-internal-path-length enumeration gives $218$.
The small constructor runs record 82,568 slot states, 145,171 suffix-level
updates, and 69,863 lower-level scans. Preprocessing, traceback, array
initialization, node deletion, and output have separate counters.

The complementary above-capacity formula is checked on 23,920 intervals
against full mixed penalty layers, and 18,000 ordinary densities agree
with their zero-gap counterparts. Those independent layer computations
examine 184,056 states and 1,300,792 root candidates.

There are 53 scale rows through perfect capacity $4095$, using positive
mixed weights with baselines $2^{60}$ on keys and $2^{61}$ on gaps.
Twenty-three smaller rows also use the independent all-root recurrence;
the larger rows check reconstructed height, direct original cost, and the
slot objective, with their independent-reference flag set to false.
At height twelve the operation counts are:

Each row tabulates 4095 valuations, builds 4095 perfect-tree nodes,

\texttt{mixed\_slots.json}.

\section{Binary-weight search and exact gap-core audit}
\label{app:binary-core}
The binary diagnostic performs 84 searches: seven power-of-two sizes, three
placements of positive weights, and four initial patterns (random,
alternating, centered, and prefix). Mutations exchange a zero and a one;
occasional reversals preserve that invariant. Mixed runs have mass $n$;
key-only and gap-only runs have mass $n/2$. The objective is the maximum
completed core across all terminal-penalty layers, including the completion
diagonal. Whole-table fixed points allow identical remaining layers to be
reported without recomputation.

The search evaluates 117,084 candidates, recording 40,476,345,560 dense
states, 80,519,888,452 root candidates, and 40,301,399,592 density evaluations.
These are diagnostic operations, not sparse-constructor costs. An independent
Python reference checks 81 vectors, including every winning vector through
$n=128$; its small cases include all-root and exhaustive-shape controls.
The largest value among the four starts in each placement is:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrrrr}
\toprule
$n$ & mixed & keys only & gaps only & proved gap upper bound\\
\midrule
16 & 69 & 50 & 44 & 44\\
32 & 182 & 137 & 100 & 100\\
64 & 465 & 341 & 228 & 228\\
128 & 1151 & 861 & 516 & 516\\
256 & 2762 & 2118 & 1156 & 1156\\
512 & 6473 & 5032 & 2564 & 2564\\
1024 & 14701 & 11571 & 5635 & 5636\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The last column follows from Theorem~\ref{thm:binary-gap-leading}; it is
an upper bound over every fixed-mass binary gap input and all layers.
The mixed and key-only columns support no asymptotic assertion.

A separate all-root recurrence checks 2,189 inputs: all binary gap vectors
through eight keys, all vectors over $\{0,1,3\}$ through five keys,
seeded nonbinary vectors, four search winners, and four explicit alternating
family members. It checks 5,282 mass identities, 32,899 one-missing-slot
formulas, 22,335 capacity densities, 157,129 vanishing above-capacity
densities, and 3,976 whole-triangle identities. The explicit family contributes
10,524 independently evaluated density formulas. Total reference work is
492,579 states and 8,892,512 root candidates. The binary bound is checked
on 60 eligible layers. Exact weights, seeds, operation counts, and source
hashes are retained in \texttt{binary\_core\_search.json} and
\texttt{gap\_core\_identity.json}.

\section{Mixed boundary-mass audit}
\label{app:mixed-boundary-mass}
An independent all-root recurrence checks 308 inputs: every mixed binary
vector through three keys, seeded integer vectors through sixteen keys,
and alternating families through $n=128$ with four baseline pairs.
The audit checks 756 mixed mass identities, 6,656 one-missing-slot formulas,

The independent all-root reference covers 272 mixed inputs: exhaustive

Sixteen scale rows use near-uniform strictly positive key and gap weights,
