\section{Capacity interaction audit}\label{app:capacity-interaction}
The independent all-root audit includes every mixed binary vector through
five keys, seeded integer inputs through 32 keys, and unit-input controls.
It checks 28,259 capacity identities, 6,416 telescoping totals, 5,856
subadditivity bounds, and 5,856 width-one interaction inequalities. Its
3,091 vectors use 371,731 states and 3,302,476 candidate roots. It also
retains the four-key wider-band counterexample from
Section~\ref{sec:wider-band-interaction}.

The separate sharp-family audit checks heights two through six, including
28 independently solved unit inputs at the smaller sizes. It verifies five
alternating-family formulas, three direct interaction differences, and five
uniform-key wider-tail formulas, using 194,152 states and 6,131,120 root
candidates. Larger rows through height 128 are closed-form evaluations only.
These records are \texttt{capacity\_interaction.json} and
\texttt{capacity\_interaction\_sharp.json}.

An initial optimized dense diagnostic investigated the proposed all-width allowance,
maximizing $[T_d(w)-\sum_xw_xT_d(e_x)]/(hW)$ over every band width and early
layer. It uses 15 fixed-mass searches through 256 keys, with 1,200 mutations
each and three starting patterns per size. Structured inputs include uniform
keys, uniform gaps, mixed weights, alternating keys, odd ramps, and seeded
random weights. Independent all-root controls check 150 optimized vectors.
Including unit-profile construction, structured evaluation uses 157,388,626
states and 313,030,555 root candidates; search uses 1,667,827,872 states and
3,310,832,256 candidates. The largest observed ratio is $1223/1536$, attained
by uniform keys. The sparse family in Theorem~\ref{thm:sparse-interaction-barrier}
subsequently disproves this allowance. Exact weights, seeds,
counters, and executed-source hashes are in
\texttt{core\_interaction\_search.json}.

\section{Sparse interaction falsification and formula audit}
\label{app:sparse-interaction}
The one-sided all-root diagnostic checks 2,956 vectors and 91,370 inequalities,
using 1,770,275 states and 28,876,151 root candidates. It finds the ten-key
above-capacity counterexample in Section~\ref{sec:wider-band-interaction}.

The subsequent sparse diagnostic evaluates 19,614 inputs, including every
unit pair on keys and gaps through 64 keys, structured unequal-weight pairs,
and sparse random inputs through 512 keys. Its optimized recurrence includes
unit-profile construction: 4,587,178,568 states, 9,062,247,119 root candidates,
and 19,197,336 band evaluations. Independent all-root controls verify the
four retained winners through 64 keys. The observed ratios exceed one and
grow with input size; these finite violations motivated the explicit family
of Theorem~\ref{thm:sparse-interaction-barrier}.

The family audit checks the combined input and both unit inputs against
independent all-root recurrences through height seven. It verifies 123,585
eligible density cells, 920 band entries, and three complete family
identities, using 870,624 states and 66,536,256 root candidates. Literal rank
choices verify 32,424 local table entries through height twelve, with
415,042 feasible rank pairs counted. Larger rows through height 128 evaluate
only the proved closed forms. These are separately labeled in
\texttt{sparse\_interaction\_barrier.json}. The other records are
\texttt{one\_sided\_interaction.json} and
\texttt{sparse\_interaction\_search.json}; all retain executed-source hashes.

\section{Rank-reward stability and expanded tail diagnostics}
\label{app:rank-reward-stability}
The rank-template audit checks 432 small inputs. Canonical maximizing
sequences are independently compared with 27,717 literal subsets over
3777 intervals. It verifies 6817 coordinate monotonicity inequalities
and 15,930 single-rank perturbation bounds. Ruler clipping is checked
on 75 larger fixtures through 256 keys, giving 45,840 inequalities;
540 upper densities are compared with independent all-root mixed
layers. The generic subsequence work totals 286,337,285 matching
candidates and 1,350,475 stored interval scores. The record is
\texttt{rank\_reward\_tail.json}.

The periodic obstruction has a separate audit through $h=7$. It checks
491 boundary entries, 20 family identities, and 11,036 upper density
cells using 2,429,031 subsequence candidates. At $h=3$, 48,620 literal
subsets independently check all 55 eligible interval scores. Larger
rows through $h=128$ evaluate only the proved closed form. These modes
are labeled in \texttt{periodic\_tail\_barrier.json}.

The original total-tail target $T_{h,d}=O(W(L+c_h/d))$ is tested
separately. A sparse diagnostic evaluates 23,183 inputs through 1024
keys, including all unit pairs on keys and gaps through 64 keys,
unequal pairs, phase-changing combs, ramps, and random small supports.
Its largest exact ratio to $W(L+c_h/d)$ is $485/168$. The optimized
recurrence records 17,157,958,100 states, 33,808,959,382 root candidates,
and 47,383,400 band evaluations. Five retained winners through 128
keys have independent all-root controls. A second diagnostic evaluates
462 repeated sparse clusters through 2048 keys; its maximum is
$2828/1107$, with 3,107,692,416 states, 5,649,382,012 root candidates,
and 3,997,440 band evaluations. Its two small retained winners also
have independent all-root controls. These finite maxima do not prove
an upper bound. The records \texttt{sparse\_tail\_search.json} and
\texttt{cluster\_tail\_search.json} retain exact weights, objectives,
operation counts, and executed-source hashes.

\section{Alternating-tail matching and sharp-scale audit}
\label{app:alternating-tail-bound}
The matching audit compares generic rank-subsequence scores with an
independent cardinality-matching recurrence on 2887 inputs. It checks
147,191 endpoint inequalities and constructs 41,517 augmenting paths
in 18,703 packings, verifying every resulting matching, removed weight,
and repair inequality. There are 58,142 global tail-bound checks.
The inputs include all binary vectors through nine keys at every
admissible odd capacity, and random integer weights through 128 keys.
Sixteen cluster instances through $C=128$ check
\eqref{eq:alternating-cluster-tail}, 1045 endpoint formulas, and 32
terminal scores. Eleven spaced-maximum families check the additive
scale. Larger cluster rows through $h=128$ use only the proved formula.
The record is \texttt{alternating\_tail\_bound.json}.

A separate compiled probe tests the wider clipped-ruler candidate
$U_d/[P(t+(c+1)/d)]$. It evaluates 25,683 inputs and records 84,957,303
interval scores, 4,737,828,651 candidate transitions, and 82,044,166
upper densities. All 451 small control vectors agree with the generic
subsequence recurrence. Its maximum observed ratio is $321/292$, on
a height-seven template with two unit keys. This disproves coefficient
one in that inequality but supplies no asymptotic upper estimate.
The exact weights, seed, counts, and source hashes are retained in
\texttt{periodic\_tail\_probe.json}.

\section{Rank-weighted repair certificates and exact barriers}
\label{app:rank-repair-barrier}
The paired canonical diagnostics each evaluate 7225 inputs, including
all ternary interior vectors on ten positions and structured, random,
and sparse inputs through 64 positions. Each run has 6976 independent
generic-subsequence controls, 224,622 endpoint checks, and 26,402 global
certificate checks. The leftmost run explicitly constructs 1,385,304
repair paths and finds 13,185 losses strictly above the true loss; the
rightmost run constructs 546,117 paths and finds 7087 strict losses.
Every path is checked for feasibility, cardinality, and its full signed
retained-rank accounting. The records are
\texttt{rank\_matching\_repair.json} and
\texttt{rightmost\_rank\_repair.json}.

The separate exact-family audit checks 3786 zero-loss equivalences on
306 inputs, and 16 instances of Lemma~\ref{lem:unique-rank-repair},
including weights above $2^{64}$, against 160 literally enumerated old
matchings and independent shortened scores. For
Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-repair-barrier}, heights three through ten give
4088 explicit rightmost boundary witnesses and repair-loss formulas,
225 independent scalar-DP witness controls, and eight complete certificate
formulas. Every optimal starting matching is enumerated at both
height-three thresholds and the largest height-four threshold, giving
1023 checks of tie-independent loss; the two height-three optimum sets
are also checked by literal enumeration without a forced-prefix assumption.
There are 32,352 explicitly checked repair paths in this family audit.

Full generic subsequence tables independently give zero true tails
through height five. Eight explicit constructions check the exact
component counts of Corollary~\ref{cor:rank-repair-components}. Larger
rows at heights 16, 32, 64, and 128 evaluate only the proved formulas.
The independent scalar controls use 41,799,787 transitions; the generic
controls use 225,793 candidate transitions and 6637 scores. Exact inputs,
operation counts, and executed-source hashes are retained in
\texttt{rank\_repair\_barrier.json}. The finite runs audit the proofs;
the unbounded ratios follow from the displayed families.

\section{Weighted cluster tails and input sensitivity diagnostics}
\label{app:weighted-cluster-tail}
For integer weights and an integer template in $[0,t]$, a right input-key
derivative is computed exactly by maximizing the pair (ordinary score,
reward at the perturbed key) lexicographically in each interval. An
independent evaluation uses
\[
 D_xU_d(p)=U_d((t+1)p+e_x)-(t+1)U_d(p).
\]
A nonzero primary-score gap is at least one; scaling by $t+1$ prevents
any slope reward, at most $t$, from closing it. Among primary maximizers,
the perturbation selects the largest slope. Taking the signed tail sum
preserves the identity. No finite-difference rounding is involved.

The weighted-cluster audit compares generic subsequence and independent
cardinality-matching tables on 81 inputs, including all ternary vectors
on a three-key cluster, unequal weights, zero minima, and ties. It checks
3652 endpoint formulas, 162 terminal scores, 169 coordinate derivatives,
and 47 joint increments. The generic recurrence uses 44,724,022 candidate
transitions and 308,926 scores; the matching recurrence uses 28,460,181
transitions and 187,123 scores. Larger formula-only rows are explicitly
labeled. The record is \texttt{weighted\_cluster\_tail.json}.

A separate full-ruler diagnostic checks 21,020 fixed-mass inputs through
256 positions and records 3,119,522,724 candidate transitions, 20,512,788
scores, and 19,108,544 density derivatives. All 29 independent scaled
perturbation controls agree with its lexicographic score/slope recurrence.
The maximum derivative ratio to $h-1+2^h/d$ is $416/129$, on 128
positions. This finite value implies no asymptotic bound or obstruction
for the full ruler. A preliminary canonical-witness probe checks 5616
Euler identities on 608 inputs and computes 11 exact directional controls.
At ties its canonical coefficients can differ from right derivatives;
these quantities remain distinct in the records
\texttt{key\_sensitivity\_search.json} and
\texttt{key\_tail\_sensitivity.json}. All records retain exact inputs,
operation counts, seeds, and executed-source hashes.

\section{Threshold falsification and boundary-tile diagnostics}
\label{app:boundary-tile-target}
The nested-threshold probe evaluates 14,432 inputs and 35,722 width
comparisons, including exhaustive ternary interior weights on ten positions.
It records 1852 upper-relation violations and 4003 reverse-relation
violations. The counterexample in Lemma~\ref{lem:nested-tail-obstruction}
is separately checked by literal enumeration of 876 subsequences for
36 interval scores, comparison with the generic subsequence recurrence,
and three original all-root terminal-layer controls. The records are
\texttt{nested\_tail\_probe.json} and
\texttt{nested\_tail\_example.json}.

The broad boundary-plus-mass diagnostic evaluates 21,436 inputs, with
structured inputs through 1024 keys, non-power-of-two sizes, sparse unit
outcomes, and fixed-mass mutations over keys, gaps, and mixed outcomes.
It scans 87,706,185 accepted tiles. Its largest observed ratio
$K(T)/(s+W(J_T))$ is $121/136$, on a gap-only input. All 93 small
independent all-root controls agree with its layerwise maxima; these
controls enumerate 15,301 tiles independently. The compiled run records
3,240,243,236 states, 6,584,091,125 root candidates, and 3,217,996,956
density evaluations. The record is \texttt{tile\_boundary\_probe.json}.

The maximum-over-all-tiles objective often settles on a small tile.
A second search therefore restricts its objective to capacity-adjacent
tiles with $s\ge4$ and $c\ge4s$, preserving total mass $n/4$ or $n$.
It evaluates 37,224 inputs through 512 keys and visits 212,592,240 tiles;
the largest eligible ratio is $11/14$. Six retained small winner tiles
agree with independent all-root layers. It records 8,185,066,752 states,
16,269,275,134 root candidates, and 8,131,873,536 density evaluations.
The record is \texttt{tile\_capacity\_support.json}. Both searches are
finite falsification diagnostics; neither proves
\eqref{eq:boundary-tile-hypothesis}.

The one-key square audit checks 1,038,774 maximal squares over all key
positions at selected sizes through 128 keys, including non-power-of-two
inputs. It also checks 1300 arbitrary squares, including non-dyadic sides,
and compares 155 full support sets against independent all-root layers.
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:one-key-square} uses only the ruler-pair
classification and interval geometry. The record is
\texttt{one\_key\_square.json}. All records retain exact inputs,
operation counts, seeds where used, and executed-source hashes.

\section{Full-ruler clipping sharpness and directed support searches}
\label{app:ruler-clipping-sharp}
The audit of Theorem~\ref{thm:ruler-clipping-sharp} enumerates feasible
ranks of the two positive keys on 16 parameter pairs, through 1024 keys.
It checks 3544 compatible score decompositions, 3544 coarse score mappings,
and 16 parity sums. Twenty generic subsequence controls independently check
full and clipped tails through 128 keys, using 2,370,068 score transitions
and 20,424 densities. Twelve original all-root controls through 64 keys
include nonzero gap weights and a key-weight scaling factor of seven;
they use 1,473,440 root candidates and 71,664 states. Literal enumeration
checks 11,520 subsequences for 18 boundary scores.

The sparse-rank boundary counts in Lemma~\ref{lem:sparse-rank-tail} have
28 independent two-key rank controls against the generic subsequence
diagnostic, plus 840 literal subsequences for 14 scores on the smallest
instance. Across both families, the rank audit uses 174,756 one-rank
candidates and 433,864 two-rank candidates for 14,276 scores. Larger
formula-only rows are marked separately. The records are
\texttt{ruler\_clipping\_sharp.json} and \texttt{spaced\_rank\_tail.json}.

A preceding joint input/template search evaluates 22,212 inputs through
128 positions. Its largest observed ratio to
$P(\max v+\sum v/d)$ is $16/17$. The explicit family in
Lemma~\ref{lem:sparse-rank-tail} instead has unbounded ratio.
The search records 865,917,888 score transitions and 9,089,940 densities;
three retained small tails have 34,560 literal-subsequence checks for
54 scores. The record is \texttt{template\_tail\_probe.json}.

A separate fixed-side, variable-integer-mass search attacks
\eqref{eq:boundary-tile-hypothesis}. It evaluates 40,557 inputs through
256 keys and visits 121,969,824 accepted tiles. Its maximum observed
ratio is $136/149$, at side 32 with 136 positive entries and local mass
117. Fifteen retained small winner tiles agree with independent all-root
layers, using 2,266,528 candidates and 109,936 states. The compiled search
records 2,806,155,040 states, 5,480,712,131 root candidates, and
2,774,094,048 density evaluations. These finite tests leave the local
support hypothesis open. The record is \texttt{tile\_support\_frontier.json}.
All records retain exact inputs, operation counts, and executed-source
hashes; seeded searches also retain their seeds.

\section{Unit-support geometry and off-envelope interactions}
\label{app:unit-support-envelope}
The single-gap density formula has 274,066 independent ordinary-density
controls, using 3,079,878 all-root candidates and 307,625 states. The
geometric audit checks 78,240 maximal dyadic tiles, 688 literal envelope
counts, 248 single-key support sets, and 1222 arbitrary squares against
the weaker $8s$ bound. The largest observed envelope-to-side ratio is
$511/256$, consistent with the proved $2s-1$ family. The record is
\texttt{unit\_support\_envelope.json}.

A directed two-outcome search excludes every envelope cell from its
support objective. It evaluates 819 inputs through 2048 keys and scans
22,696,956 tiles. Its counts are 3,760,248,528 states, 7,001,021,069 root
candidates, and 3,755,113,776 density evaluations. Six retained small
winner tiles agree with independent all-root layers, using 718,432
candidates and 35,984 states. This search suggested the explicit family;
the asymptotic obstruction follows from
Theorem~\ref{thm:off-envelope-interaction}. The record is
\texttt{interaction\_support\_probe.json}.

The explicit-family audit checks $s=8,16,\ldots,1024$, hence
$n=64,128,\ldots,8192$. It verifies 36 density-one witnesses and 144
four-corner score formulas by independent feasible-rank enumeration,
using 23,808 single-rank and 2,090,940 pair-rank candidates.
At $n\le128$, twelve scores also agree with a generic subsequence
recurrence, using 302,446 transitions and 2806 score entries; three
densities agree with original all-root layers, using 2,375,360 root
candidates and 59,936 states. Larger cases use feasible-rank enumeration,
rather than a dense all-root computation. The record is
\texttt{unit\_envelope\_obstruction.json}. All three records retain
executed-source SHA-256 manifests. Verification counts do not enter
the proofs of the gap formula, geometric envelope, or interaction family.

\section{Pair amplification and exact binary dilation}
\label{app:binary-dilation}
The fixed-tile amplification search evaluates 2470 inputs through 1024
keys, including translated pairs, shared endpoints, unequal weights,
and local integer-weight mutations. It records 1,858,339,360 states,
3,315,818,765 root candidates, and 6,790,720 density evaluations.
Six retained tiles have independent all-root controls using 7,126,080
candidates and 179,808 states. The largest observed ratio
$K(T)/(s+W(J_T))$ is $12/25$; no upper bound follows from this finite
maximum. The record is \texttt{pair\_amplification\_probe.json}.

The independent binary-dilation audit checks saturation on 1536 cases
using 21,588 literal subsequences, with arbitrary nonnegative rank rewards.
The two scope counterexamples use twelve additional literal subsequences.
Forty dilation instances give 6142 score controls and 4392 density controls
from separate generic subsequence recurrences, using 416,654 transitions
and 8958 score entries. Original all-root layers with arbitrary nonzero
gap weights check 2286 entries and 1434 densities, using 398,656 root
candidates and 38,304 states. All 42 positive controlled fine densities
are outside the unit-outcome envelope. Rounded endpoints inside zero
blocks are included. The record is \texttt{binary\_dilation\_audit.json}.

A larger original-layer audit checks 27 replicated pair, alternating,
and three-block patterns through 1024 keys. Every instance preserves
the coarse tile's core cardinality and density mass; for $d>1$, every
positive fine density is off-envelope. This run uses 22,874,208 states,
40,616,815 root candidates, and 84,672 density evaluations. It uses
monotone roots; the preceding audit supplies independent all-root controls
at smaller sizes. The record is \texttt{binary\_dilation\_family.json}.
All three records retain executed-source SHA-256 manifests. The exact
identity and support transport follow from Section~\ref{sec:binary-dilation},
independently of these finite checks.


\section{Plateau cores and rank-threshold compatibility}
\label{app:plateau-core}
The endpoint-cut search evaluates 1960 inputs through 1024 keys, including
replicated coarse patterns, unequal blocks, positive endpoint fringes,
and integer-weight mutations. It records 1,707,458,304 states,
3,241,769,152 root candidates, and 6,204,928 density evaluations.
Fourteen retained tiles have independent all-root controls using
16,627,520 candidates and 419,552 states. The largest observed ratio
$K(T)/(s+W(J_T))$ is $20/37$. This finite maximum proves no upper
bound. The record is \texttt{endpoint\_amplification\_probe.json}.

The one-block audit checks 5561 complete above-capacity support sets
against the original all-root recurrence, including nonzero gap weights
and positive endpoint cuts, through 64 keys. Its 1,143,360 density
controls include 635,883 positive-endpoint zero identities. The original
recurrence uses 33,319,148 root candidates and 2,723,869 states.
Independent literal visibility enumeration checks 193,533 pairs;
23,375 block rewards agree with both direct ruler sums and
\eqref{eq:plateau-reward}. Constructed support sets include the exact
values, not only cardinalities. The local square corollary has 50,032
controls, exhaustive through twelve keys and sampled at larger sizes.
Twelve larger sharp-family constructions
reach 32,768 keys; these use the linear constructor rather than the
original recurrence. At the largest size it computes 16,383 ruler values,
performs 16,382 sliding updates, pushes 16,383 and pops 16,369 stack
indices, considers 32,738 pairs, and outputs 16,356 densities.
The record is \texttt{plateau\_core\_audit.json}.

\paragraph{The complementary below-capacity construction.}\label{app:plateau-below}
The independent audit covers 1025 block inputs: every contiguous block through
17 keys, selected cases through 64 keys, rational common weights, and heights
through twice the binary length of $n$. It compares 8812 complete cores with
the original all-root recurrence, checking 1,070,212 ordinary densities,
88,033 top-sum interval values, and 222,434 local tiles. The top-sum identity
also has 336 arbitrary-template controls with literal subset enumeration.
The original references use 18,032,986 root candidates and 1,556,996 states.
Sixteen larger sparse constructions reach 65,536 keys and enumerate every
layer through twice the binary length. Their checks use the explicit coordinate
identities, without a large original all-root reference. Counts and final
source hashes are in \texttt{plateau\_below\_audit.json}.

The separate fixed-tile search maximizes $K(T)-W(J_T)$, using variable integer
mass with key-only, gap-only, and mixed mutations on both sides of capacity.
It evaluates 34,692 inputs through 640 keys, using 22,971,840 tile-density
checks and 11,439,157,710 optimized root candidates. Twenty-seven small
winners agree with independent all-root layers, using 9,000,132 candidates.
The maximum observed normalized excess is $39/64$, from $K=63$, $W=24$,
$s=64$. The record \texttt{tile\_excess\_probe.json} retains full winners,
counts, seed, and source hashes. These finite results do not prove the proposed
$K=O(s+W)$ bound; Theorem~\ref{thm:plateau-below} has an independent proof.


The rank-threshold discovery probe evaluates 65,454 inputs using
15,176,940 subsequence transitions. Its first obstruction has 22 keys,
full reward seven, and threshold optima $(5,2,1)$. Independent literal
enumeration checks all 170,544 length-15 subsequences of that input.
The explicit family in Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-threshold-obstruction} and
the smaller example are checked by all 32,752 single deletions through
16,384 keys, evaluating 425,838 retained positive ranks and 5,176,333
threshold comparisons. For $n\le128$, 22 independent generic subsequence
controls use 145,386 transitions and 66 score entries; four original
all-root controls with nonzero gaps use 2,812,064 root candidates and
73,456 states. The larger rows enumerate every deletion and are not
original all-root runs. The record is
\texttt{rank\_threshold\_obstruction.json}, which also hashes the raw
search artifact. All three audit records retain executed-source
SHA-256 manifests. The theorems follow from their proofs independently
of these finite checks.


\clearpage
\section{Finite band products and complete two-block cores}
\label{app:two-block-core}
The generic band audit checks 1332 inputs, including exhaustive binary
sequences through $N=4$ and signed integer sequences through $N=16$.
It compares 28,024 finite product entries against 2,098,072 literal pair
choices, including infeasible completion entries, and checks 23,821
feasible rewards. A further 3996 coarsened-core and 11,808 coarsened-product
entry controls test the local aggregation in Lemma~\ref{lem:band-dominance}.
These checks test exact values and the finite penalties, not only the
support constants.

The interior audit checks 576 support sets on 168 mixed inputs through
32 keys against original all-root layers, with 5690 densities and 672
literal subsequence intervals from 20,517 choices. Its original recurrence
uses 1,345,248 root candidates and 146,592 states. Twenty-four larger
condensed constructions reach 16,384 keys, with 661 additional square
controls. Those rows are constructor checks rather than original all-root
runs. The record is \texttt{band\_dominance\_audit.json}.

The complete audit checks 952 mixed inputs through 64 keys, including
all 792 binary words with exactly two positive runs through ten keys and
160 seeded larger inputs with common positive key weight three. Nonzero
gap weights are included. All 2953 complete above-capacity support sets
and 334,032 densities agree with the independent all-root recurrence,
which uses 12,507,215 root candidates and 784,506 states.
Fourteen larger full constructions reach 8192 keys and include 864 local
square controls. For two unit keys at that size, the constructor evaluates
31,017 distinct density candidates and outputs 3219 positive densities.
It performs 26,527 range queries and 15,631 condensed-matrix value queries;
the record separately counts the reused sparse-product operations. These
larger rows do not have original all-root references. The record is
\texttt{two\_block\_core\_audit.json}. Both records retain executed-source
SHA-256 manifests. The bounds follow from the finite product, visibility,
and endpoint-case proofs, independently of the finite audits.


\clearpage
\section{Finite rank transfers and dense-core audit}
\label{app:rank-transfer}
The audit exhausts 363 inputs over key weights $\{0,1,3\}$ through five
keys. It compares 23,232 finite transfer entries, including infeasible
completion entries, with independent dense products using 459,456 candidate
additions. All 11,385 feasible entries also agree with a separate subsequence
DP and 52,245 literal subsequences. Thirty-two rational-weight inputs add
1048 exact entry controls.

For 70 mixed inputs through 32 keys, including geometric, increasing,
near-uniform, constant, and random key weights with nonzero gap weights,
1172 original all-root layer scores agree with
\eqref{eq:rank-transfer-layer}. Separately, 32 many-block inputs with varied
positive-block lengths have 128 original-layer support-set comparisons and
3544 interior density controls. Together these original all-root controls
use 987,840 root candidates and 104,460 states.

One hundred chains of signed unary functions supply 3850 feasible-score
controls against an independent state DP. The explicit width-one family
in Theorem~\ref{thm:band-chain-dense} has 1493 formula entries checked
against both that DP and condensed products, and 341 density-one square
cells. Six weighted-ramp constructions through $c=255$ check 33,138
transfer formula entries and 32,136 densities from
Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-transfer-dense}. The four smaller rows, through
$c=63$, also have 2130 independent subsequence-DP entry controls. The two
larger rows use the condensed product and the closed form, without that
additional DP control. At $c=255$, $r=128$, the strict feasible strip has
12,097 positive entries and the side-64 square has 2048. The product
has 127 chain multiplications, whose total input-core count is 206,530;
its internal multiplication operations are recorded separately.

The record is \texttt{rank\_transfer\_audit.json}. It preserves exact
operation counts and executed-source SHA-256 manifests. These finite
checks verify the implementations and identities at the recorded sizes;
the dense families and construction bounds follow from the proofs.


\paragraph{Slot-window products and full prescribed-height trees.}
The independent slot-window audit exhausts 363 inputs over $\{0,1,3\}$
through five keys. All 23,232 finite product entries, including infeasible
completion entries, agree with separately evaluated dense products using
459,456 candidate additions. The 2946 feasible entries also agree with an
independent weighted-subsequence DP and 20,340 literal subsequences.
Twenty-five rational single-run cases, including runs longer than capacity,
check another 6820 finite factor entries.

One hundred mixed inputs through 24 keys use constant internal gaps,
arbitrary endpoint gaps, and uniform, geometric, zero, or random keys.
The complete constructor agrees with 200 original all-root height optima;
every output tree has independently checked inorder, height, and original
mixed cost. The references use 235,160 root candidates and 34,270 states.
The audit also checks 220 insufficient-capacity rejections and the explicit
rejection of unequal internal gaps.

Nine checkerboard squares through side 512 verify 349,524 four-corner
transition densities against \eqref{eq:slot-gap-checkerboard}. Eight larger
uniform or three-run constructions through 8192 keys include the sparse
product, witness traceback, full tree repair, and independent output-tree
cost and height. Uniform rows additionally match the closed minimum-IPL
optimum; the larger three-run rows have no independent dense optimum
reference. At 8192 keys and height 14, the three-run construction has
61,431 input core entries, two run-product multiplications, and a final
core of 20,147 entries. Leaf traceback scans the 16,383 slots twice in
total, once for buckets and once for output. The record
\texttt{slot\_run\_audit.json} contains exact operation counts and seven
executed-source hashes. The theoretical bounds and signed obstruction
follow from the proofs above.


\paragraph{Vector construction with arbitrary mixed gaps.}
The subsequent audit exhausts all 680 mixed binary inputs through four
keys. Literal enumeration checks 7880 tree-cost normalization identities.
Independent prefix enumeration checks 12,496 state values and their
attaining predecessors using 53,824 selected-slot sequences. Forty-eight
finite forced-end factors supply 15,600 entry comparisons with the prior
condensed window factor and 14,256 four-corner Monge controls, including
infeasible entries and rational weights.

Another 84 mixed inputs through 32 keys include geometric weights,
unequal gaps, rational weights, long key runs and isolated gap changes.
Together the two input groups give 1528 comparisons with the original
all-root height optimum, each with independent output-tree order, height
and original mixed cost, plus 1184 insufficient-capacity checks. The
reference recurrence uses 828,560 root candidates and 107,736 states.

Twelve larger complete constructions through 8192 keys cover uniform keys,
three key runs, and three key runs with exceptional internal and endpoint
gaps. Uniform rows additionally match the closed minimum-IPL optimum;
the other larger rows have output-tree checks but no independent large
optimum oracle. At 8192 keys and height 14, the case with three exceptional
internal gaps has five transformed key runs, six long-run passes and five
singleton passes. It makes 752,788 finite run-entry queries, processes
61,437 singleton states, and stores 180,224 predecessor slots. The record
\texttt{slot\_vector\_audit.json} retains the separate exact counters and
eleven executed-source hashes. SMAWK is the published primitive cited in
Theorem~\ref{thm:slot-vector}; the full construction bound follows from
its finite Monge interface and the valuation-sum amortization.

\paragraph{Sufficient unrestricted height and full vector construction.}
The height audit exhausts 22,140 mixed inputs over $\{0,1,9\}$ through
four keys. A further 72 inputs through eight keys bring the literal shape
count to 323,604, including 30,955 optimal shapes. The audit computes
$\mu$ and $z$ independently from every interval. It checks 152,072 sorted
marked-set cuts and 790,245 positive-subtree depth assertions across the
optimal shapes. Separately, 287,760 arbitrary marked subsets through three
keys check the disjoint-charge bound without choosing the largest weights.

There are 6454 complete constructor controls against shape or original
all-root optima, including rational scaling, geometric weights, isolated
positive outcomes and zero-mass intervals; three empty inputs are checked
separately. The all-root references use 1,357,542 root candidates. Twelve
additional full vector constructions through 384 keys use three key runs
and moderate or arbitrarily large exceptional key/gap weights. Every one
matches an independent ordinary Knuth optimum and has its original tree
cost, order and height checked. These twelve runs deliberately force the
vector kernel: at these sizes the work selector would choose Knuth.

For the 384-key row with one $2^{192}$ key and one $2^{128}$ gap, the
untrimmed sufficient height is 278; marking those two outcomes gives
height 18, with remaining mass 767 and $\mu=1$. The full forced kernel
makes 12,489,698 finite run-entry queries, processes 524,018 singleton
states, and stores 1,835,008 predecessor slots. This verifies the
construction and height theorem, and does not establish a practical
speed advantage at that size. A separate 1024-key geometric input checks
iterative reconstruction of the complete rightmost-root chain against
its endpoint-dominance optimum $2^{1025}-1026$.

Twelve height-only plans with two exceptional outcomes and background
keys in $\{1,2,3\}$ give heights 20, 23, 26 and 29 at sizes 1024, 4096,
16,384 and 65,536. Exceptional exponents 64/80 and 4096/4112 give the
same budgets; no full construction is run at these sizes. A separate
all-root audit checks 145,424 intervals across 1552 original or globally
normalized inputs, using 2,551,952 root candidates and retaining minimum
height among tied optimal costs. Rational and large weights are included.
The records \texttt{trimmed\_height\_audit.json} and
\texttt{trimmed\_interval\_audit.json} retain separate operation counters,
reference scopes, and seven and three executed-source hashes, respectively.


\paragraph{Valuation-class records and sparse full-tree reconstruction.}
The record audit first checks every nonempty slot subset through height
three: 135 subsets and 461 selected-key depth inequalities. A separate
6231-window control checks exact highest-valuation slot selection and
earliest ties through height five, without scanning in the constructor.

All 680 binary mixed inputs through four keys, together with 60 further
mixed, rational, geometric and run-structured inputs through 32 keys,
give 1540 full constructions at prescribed heights. Every one matches
both the dense prefix-vector optimum and an independent original all-root
height recurrence. The audit checks all 226,280 prefix values and their
attaining compressed predecessors, plus exact equality of the retained
breakpoint sets at 6778 stage boundaries. At small heights, 53,824 literal
slot selections provide a third prefix-value control. The original
all-root reference uses 881,564 root candidates.

Eight larger complete constructions through 1024 keys cover a zero-weight
middle run and genuinely mixed residual gaps. Their selected sufficient
heights certify unrestricted optimality, and all match independent ordinary
Knuth optima and direct output-tree cost, order and height checks. At
1024 keys, the zero-middle row uses height 25, implicit capacity
33,554,431, and 93,615 retained states across three stages. It makes
8,405,286 finite factor queries and 201,726,864 threshold-term evaluations;
the repaired ancestor tree has 1034 vertices. These counts are distinct
operation categories, rather than a wall-time comparison.

Three more full constructions keep 16 uniform keys while prescribing
heights 16, 32 and 64. They all return cost 54, matching the closed
minimum-internal-path-length optimum. At height 64 the implicit capacity is
$2^{64}-1$, while the constructor retains 15,120 prefix states, queries
302,820 destination rows, and constructs only 16 ancestor vertices. These
rows check the absence of perfect-tree expansion; the integer complexity
bound follows from the score-range proof, not from their measured sizes.
The record \texttt{slot\_records\_audit.json} retains separate operation
counts, reference scopes and seven executed-source hashes.

The marked-spectrum audit checks 60 inputs through five keys by enumerating
1428 slot selections, including rational marked weights and integer
unmarked weights. Six further complete unrestricted mixed constructions
through 192 keys place one exceptional key and one exceptional gap among
three integer background runs. Gap exponents 64 and 512, paired with key
exponents 80 and 528, all match independent ordinary Knuth optima.
At 192 keys, both exponent choices select height 17 and retain the same
45,729 records across five stages; sparse repair constructs exactly 192
vertices. These equal counts are an observed control, while
\eqref{eq:slot-record-marked-count} proves the magnitude-independent bound.
The separate \texttt{slot\_marked\_records\_audit.json} retains six source hashes.


\paragraph{Exact exponential prefix-record support.}
The universal envelope in Lemma~\ref{lem:slot-record-envelope} has
three independent controls. Earliest valuation-preserving embeddings are
checked on all 32,902 nonempty slot subsets at heights one through four,
covering 246,221 selected positions. Seventy-two arbitrary mixed rational
inputs, including large exceptional keys, check 12,424 retained records
against the envelope and compare 18,088 prefix values with the dense mixed
recurrence; every complete output tree is independently checked. Finally,
for each height $h=1,\ldots,6$ and every key count $1\le n\le2^h-1$,
geometric weights of base $h+1$ attain the envelope at all prefixes:
2,667 stage sets and 97,155 dense states are checked, including $k>h$.

The positive mixed family in Theorem~\ref{thm:slot-record-barrier} is checked
by a dense subsequence recurrence that retains every ending slot, independently
of the compressed kernel. Across sizes 2 through 16, all 1,965,265 prefix
values and attaining compressed predecessors agree. The same states check
the popcount support test and the exact strict-increase locations of prefix
maxima. All 135 complete record sets agree with the binomial formula.
A separate enumeration checks 65,519 explicit subset-of-scales witnesses.
Through five keys, 208,376 literal slot selections provide another reference;
2055 literal BST shapes through eight keys verify the unique full optimum.
Nine complete record-kernel trees through ten keys agree with that optimum.

At 16 keys, the middle prefix with eight keys has exactly 39,195 records;
the final prefix has 65,520. The 135 measured stage counts also satisfy
the summed identity in \eqref{eq:slot-record-barrier-count}. Thirty further
ascending, descending and shuffled magnitude-order diagnostics through 12
keys retain their counts without implying a universal upper bound.
Formula evaluations at sizes 32 through 1024 are labeled separately;
no exponential prefix enumeration is run at those sizes.

Six complete constructions of the same family by the existing affine solver
reach 1024 keys. Every result matches its closed unique-chain cost and has
all output links, order, depths and original mixed cost checked. Independent
Knuth references are used only through 256 keys. At 1024 keys, the affine
constructor uses exactly 2047 root candidates and returns the complete
chain; the largest input weight has 10,242 bits. This checks the separation
between explicit-record size and full-problem difficulty. The record
\texttt{slot\_record\_barrier.json} retains exact counters, reference scopes,
and eight executed-source hashes. No timing supports the theorem.

\paragraph{Implicit mixed prefix diagrams.}
The additive-diagram constructor is compared with a direct all-predecessor
prefix recurrence on 72,880 entries, including infeasible positions. All
1392 complete small trees agree with an independent original all-root
height recurrence and have every key, gap, order constraint, and depth
checked. The inputs include all 680 binary mixed instances through four
keys at two heights, plus rational and geometric controls through 24 keys.
The direct prefix reference evaluates 486,336 predecessor candidates;
these are reference operations, separate from constructor work.

Five complete geometric constructions use the general diagram solver at
necessary height $h=n$, for $n=8,16,24,32,48$. Each agrees with the closed
unique-chain optimum and independent ordinary Knuth. At 48 keys the final
function has 45,391 reachable diagram nodes, while its explicit record
count is $2^{48}-48$. Construction creates 3,069,517 nonterminal nodes,
keeps 1,145,477 nodes reachable from all prefix roots, and makes 3,282,551
minimum calls, 2,290,986 addition calls, and 2,200,220 prefix calls.
The large difference between final and intermediate sizes is retained
explicitly. A 64-key scale run was interrupted and supplies no completed
row or complexity evidence. The source-hashed record is
\texttt{slot\_dag\_audit.json}; no timing is used.

A separate prefix-operation probe checks 108 signed functions with arbitrary
infeasibility patterns at heights one through nine, comparing all 12,264
positions and checking the stated linear-in-$hD$ operation bounds.
The monotone-minimum family is checked for $m=1,\ldots,12$: every
normalized middle-level node is counted, and the total is exactly
$3\cdot2^m-2$. There are 45,744 address controls of both operands and
their minimum, exhaustive through $m=6$ and sampled at the proved kink
boundaries thereafter. The formula has the independent proof in
Theorem~\ref{thm:dag-minimum-barrier}.

Nine additional unrestricted mixed constructions use certified sufficient
heights and match ordinary Knuth plus complete output-tree checks. They
include random and near-uniform keys and gaps through 64 keys, and
geometric keys with nonconstant, nonaffine gaps through 16 keys. The
64-key random row has height 11, 413 final nodes, and 187,957 total
created nodes including its terminal; the near-uniform row has height 10,
230 final nodes, and 50,714 total nodes. At 16 keys the nonaffine
geometric row has height 16, 1283 final nodes, and 199,887 total nodes.
These complete controls establish correctness on the displayed inputs;
the measured sizes supply no universal graph-size bound. The record
\texttt{slot\_dag\_probe.json} retains input weights, operation counters,
reference counts, and source hashes.

\paragraph{Actual-prefix size search and skyline traceback.}
A directed search uses exact dense mixed prefix values, independently
normalizes every dyadic block, and maximizes the largest reachable diagram
across all stages. It evaluates 204 inputs at $n=h=6,8,10,12,14,16$,
using shuffled geometric keys, geometric gaps, and local coefficient or
swap mutations. Every full optimum agrees with ordinary Knuth. The
largest sizes found are respectively $58,157,310,548,1335,2479$;
the corresponding final sizes are $49,81,140,311,341,1920$. Each winner
has every prefix entry independently compared with the implicit constructor.
Height $n$ is sufficient on these inputs; the search does not certify
that it is necessary. These finite sizes imply no growth exponent.
The record is \texttt{slot\_dag\_size\_search.json}.

A second search uses positive mixed inputs with necessary height $n$.
For $A=8(n+1)^2$, choose coefficients $\alpha_i,\beta_j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}$
and set
\[
 p_i=\alpha_i A^{n-i},\qquad
 q_j=\beta_j A^{\max(0,n-j-1)}
 \quad(0\le i<n,\ 0\le j\le n).
\]
For a suffix beginning at $i$, put $r=n-i$ and let $R$ be the mass of
all its outcomes other than $p_i$. Then
$R<2nA^r/(A-1)+n$, so
$nR/p_i<3n^2/(A-1)<1$. The right chain has suffix cost at most
$p_i+nR$, while a different root incurs at least $2p_i$.
Recursion forces the unique right chain and hence height $n$.
This argument applies to every allowed coefficient mutation.
At the same six sizes, 48 inputs are evaluated. The largest found
sizes, also the winners' final sizes, are $48,123,266,495,829,1283$.
Every candidate has an independent Knuth optimum check; each winning
complete skyline construction agrees with the closed chain cost and has
all links, gaps, order constraints, and depths checked. The source-hashed
record is \texttt{slot\_dag\_chain\_search.json}.

The separate skyline audit checks 42,144 prefix values against direct
all-predecessor minimization, 168,576 predecessor queries against literal
candidate scans, and 185,856 endpoint-dominance inequalities. It includes
all 680 binary mixed inputs through four keys and 24 rational inputs
through 12 keys, totaling 704 complete all-root optimum and tree checks.
Queries use zero, integer, and rational nonnegative gap prices, and include
infeasible prefixes and the global endpoint sentinel. Six complete
constructions on the unconstrained search winners agree with Knuth and
all independently counted stage sizes. Their slot traceback makes exactly
$n^2$ path steps at $h=n$ and creates zero diagram nodes, as predicted
by Theorem~\ref{thm:dag-skyline-query}. The record
\texttt{slot\_dag\_skyline\_audit.json} retains operation counts, executed
source hashes, and the hash of its consumed search record.

\paragraph{Scalar-carry forward construction.}
The independent forward audit checks every one of 60,336 prefix entries
against direct predecessor minimization, including infeasible positions.
All 680 binary mixed inputs through four keys are checked at both the
minimum feasible height and one larger height; 24 rational inputs through
12 keys add nonintegral controls. All 1384 complete trees agree with the
original all-root height recurrence and have their links, order, depths,
and original key/gap costs independently verified.

The same harness separately constructs the inclusive prefix-minimum
diagram and checks 15,993 expanded carry contexts. For each actual
non-pruned dyadic block it verifies that the inclusive-prefix minimum
is the input block minimum, and that the normalized first-address value
is exactly the relative carry, including infinity. Every executed context
is covered, and distinct contexts have distinct input/prefix node pairs.
This checks the earlier, weaker $O(hD^2)$ state bound by node-pair
injection. The sharper reset-path proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:dag-forward}
applies to the same unchanged constructor and is audited separately below.

Twelve further full constructions reuse the retained winners of both
size searches. Every stage has exactly the independently counted diagram
size, and every complete optimum agrees with Knuth and output-tree checks.
On the 16-key unconstrained winner, the scalar-carry constructor creates
14,124 nodes versus 144,500 in the previous construction of that same
input; it expands 3829 carry contexts. On the 16-key necessary-chain
winner, the respective counts are 19,090 and 199,603 nodes, with 10,110
carry contexts. All twelve new constructions make zero calls to generic
pointwise diagram minimum, addition, or prefix operations. The record
\texttt{slot\_dag\_forward\_audit.json} retains exact counters, nine executed
source hashes, and hashes of both consumed search records.

A separate scale audit constructs positive mixed inputs from the
strict-dominance chain family above, with random coefficients and necessary
height $h=n$. Each full tree agrees with its closed unique-chain cost and
ordinary Knuth; all links, depths, keys, and gaps are checked. Its exact
counts are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{r|r r r r}
$n$ & final nodes & all created nodes & history nodes & carry contexts\\
\hline
24 & 4928 & 106914 & 65358 & 58124\\
32 & 12410 & 358192 & 215203 & 197331\\
48 & 45391 & 1939134 & 1145477 & 1081655\\
64 & 111668 & 6355399 & 3722796 & 3567441
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The all-created count includes the terminal; history counts nodes reachable
from all retained prefix roots. At 64 keys there are also 38,536,277
first-address replacement visits, retained rather than hidden by the
smaller final diagram. No timing or measured exponent supports the bound.
The record \texttt{slot\_dag\_forward\_scale.json} retains every original
weight, eight source hashes, and all operation categories. These inputs
are distinct from the earlier interrupted 64-key experiment.

\paragraph{Reset paths and exact generic sharpness.}
A separate audit checks the reset-path cover of
Lemma~\ref{lem:dag-carry-reset} on 1416 updates, examining all 34,186
expanded states and 18,513 reset edges. For each update it reconstructs
inherited and reset edges, checks at most one inherited child, checks that
each parent input node determines a single reset seed, and verifies that
the seed paths cover exactly the expanded states. It checks both the
unweighted and depth-weighted bounds, every node-assembly and replacement
count in \eqref{eq:dag-reset-work}, the recursive call identity, and the
summary bounds. No generic pointwise minimum, addition, or prefix call is
made.

There are 148 arbitrary-diagram controls, including signed rational and
infinite entries, with four compact inputs at heights 16, 32, 64 and 128.
They test the counting lemma only. Separately, 372 complete mixed trees
are verified: all 168 binary inputs through three keys at two heights,
24 rational inputs through 16 keys, and the twelve retained size-search
winners. The first 360 have original all-root height references; the
winners have independent Knuth references and independently counted stage
sizes. Every complete tree is checked against its original weights.
The record \texttt{slot\_dag\_reset\_audit.json} retains all counters, nine
executed-source hashes, and both consumed search-record hashes.

The sharpness audit checks the exact closed formulas of
Theorem~\ref{thm:dag-reset-sharp} for $1\le m\le16$ and for
$m\in\{24,32,48,64\}$.
It examines 14,700 expanded contexts and checks 7085 input values, exhaustively
through $m=6$ and with labeled samples thereafter. At $m=64$, the 129-node
input at height 128 causes exactly 6240 expanded contexts and 316,096
node-assembly calls, with the same number of replacement visits.
Every row also checks the explicit endpoint-dominance violation. These
are general-diagram operation controls, not complete OBST constructions.
The record \texttt{slot\_dag\_reset\_sharp.json} retains seven source hashes.
The closed formulas supply the asymptotic tightness independently of these
finite checks.

\paragraph{Punctured diagrams and complete mixed construction.}
The new representation is checked against all 60,336 direct prefix entries
and 94,640 direct predecessor queries with integer and rational gap prices.
All 1384 small complete trees agree with the original all-root height
recurrence: every binary input through four keys at two feasible heights,
plus 24 rational inputs through 12 keys. Every output tree's original
key/gap cost, order, links, and depths are checked.

On 96 arbitrary signed/rational/infinite diagrams, conversion agrees with
independent dense canonicalization and all 6024 nonzero-address values.
These inputs check representation and operation counts only. Twelve
retained adversarial mixed inputs additionally have every prefix compared
by canonical equality with conversion of the earlier ordinary-diagram
constructor. Their complete trees agree with independent Knuth optima.
Across these controls and the generic sharp family, 5456 updates check all
59,358 expanded states and 26,108 reset edges, exact reset-path coverage,
$Z+T$ assembly calls, $2Z+1$ recursive calls, and the summary bounds.
The sharp family checks Corollary~\ref{cor:punctured-sharp} through $m=64$,
including its exclusion from the OBST domain. At $m=64$ its 128-node input
requires 6240 expanded states and exactly 6303 assembly calls.

The sorting-free repair is separately checked on every one of 32,902
nonempty slot subsets through height four. Its complete output tree equals
the retained sorting-based repair in every case; the ancestor traversal
makes exactly two visits per collected vertex. This checks the replacement
of sorting by traversal independently of the optimizer.
The record \texttt{slot\_punctured\_audit.json} retains all counts, ten
source hashes, and both consumed size-search hashes.

A separate full-construction audit uses the identical necessary-height
mixed chain inputs of the earlier scale audit. Each constructed tree
matches the unique-chain formula, independent Knuth, and all original
weights. Exact counts for the punctured representation are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{r|r r r}
$n=h$ & final nodes & all created nodes & assembly calls\\
\hline
24 & 3054 & 42092 & 58424\\
32 & 7806 & 143956 & 197859\\
48 & 29143 & 795874 & 1082831\\
64 & 72235 & 2636579 & 3569521
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The same 64-key input previously created 6,355,399 ordinary nodes and
performed 38,536,341 assembly calls and 38,536,277 first-address replacement
visits, including the initial ordinary point diagram in the assembly count. The new constructor performs no first-address replacements.
These are matched exact operation counts; the theorem supplies the bound.

Four additional arbitrary mixed integer inputs at 32, 64, 128 and 256 keys
use the proved sufficient height selector and agree with independent
Knuth and complete-tree checks. The selected heights are 11, 13, 14 and 16.
At 256 keys the final punctured diagram has 460 nodes; complete construction
creates 73,226 nodes and makes 114,633 assembly calls. All intermediate
work is retained, and these measurements assert no all-stage size bound.
The record \texttt{slot\_punctured\_scale.json} retains every original
weight, six source hashes, and the consumed earlier scale-record hash.

\paragraph{Actual-prefix cubic and shared-history quartic witnesses.}
The audit for Theorem~\ref{thm:punctured-size-barrier} first compares every
greedy ending-slot reward with an independent dense subsequence recurrence
for $2\le n\le12$, giving 89,749 exact entry comparisons. Eight complete
constructions at $n=12,13,16,17,24,25,32,48$ then check all three entries
of every witness at every prefix. There are 507,376 witnessed nodes and
1,522,128 entry comparisons. Each witness also checks the first and last
nonzero coefficient positions and the coefficient identifying $s$.
Actual canonical node identities are distinct across all prefix roots,
so the history test includes full cross-stage sharing. Odd sizes check
the even probe offset $Q=2\lceil n/2\rceil$ separately from $Q=n$.

Every full output agrees with independent Knuth, the closed chain cost,
and original-weight tree checks; all 187 suffix dominance certificates
hold. The finite node counts and proved lower bounds are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{r|r r|r r}
$n$ & final nodes & final lower bound & shared history & history lower bound\\
\hline
12 & 287 & 56 & 2044 & 434\\
13 & 379 & 84 & 2912 & 714\\
16 & 767 & 165 & 7242 & 1815\\
17 & 937 & 220 & 9429 & 2585\\
24 & 3054 & 969 & 42092 & 16473\\
25 & 3488 & 1140 & 50176 & 20235\\
32 & 7806 & 2600 & 143956 & 60450\\
48 & 29143 & 11480 & 795874 & 404670
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The record \texttt{slot\_punctured\_barrier.json} retains every stage count,
all operation counts and seven source hashes. Rows from 64 through 4096
keys evaluate only the proved formulas; they are not enumerations or full
constructions. The lower-bound proof is independent of these measurements.

\paragraph{Positive affine block sharing.}
The audit for Theorem~\ref{thm:affine-diagram-barrier} checks all 43,941
unordered quadratic exponents at selected sizes through $n=256$ for
uniqueness. It then compares 18,213 greedy scores with independent dense
subsequence values at $2\le n\le10$. Five complete constructions at
$n=8,12,13,16,17$ check every witness's three entries against the actual
prefix function: 5567 distinct affine invariants and 16,701 exact entry
comparisons. Every invariant is also checked after two nontrivial positive
affine transformations, giving 11,134 exact rational controls. Distinctness
is checked across all prefix stages, and the coefficient tests verify
linear independence and recovery of $r,s,k$.

All five original-weight trees agree with ordinary Knuth and the closed
chain cost, with 66 strict suffix certificates. The largest normalized
weight has 10,322, 38,740, 50,247, 98,499 and 119,841 bits in the five
rows, respectively. These finite bit lengths are reported separately from
arithmetic-operation counts. The record \texttt{slot\_affine\_barrier.json}
retains each exponent sequence, stage counts, exact operation counts and
eight source hashes. The existing additive solver supplies the tested
function values; no affine-normalized solver is claimed or required for
the equivalence-class lower bound.

\paragraph{Lazy search, valid bounds, and forced exponential states.}
The audit for Lemma~\ref{lem:lazy-rearrangement} exhausts the 680 binary
mixed inputs through four keys and adds 48 seeded rational inputs at
$n=5,8,12$. At two heights per input it checks 55,744 bound entries
against dense exact prefix values and independently sorted dot products.
It checks all 14,245 evaluated prefix states against fresh all-predecessor
recurrences and all 1456 height-constrained optima against the original
all-root recurrence. Another 2184 complete constructions use budgets
zero, seventeen, and the default $8n^2$. All original-weight tree costs
match unrestricted Knuth. Of these, 2174 use fallback and ten finish the
lazy search; every exhaustion occurs at exactly one counted operation
beyond its budget. The record \texttt{slot\_lazy\_audit.json} retains
these counts, seed and eight source hashes.

The matched scale audit retains 22 complete constructions with independent
Knuth controls. Five uniform rows at $n=32,64,128,256,512$ finish the lazy
search, expanding exactly $n$ states each. Their counted search work is
5290, 12,756, 30,263, 87,117 and 199,099 operations, respectively. All ten
bounded and binary mixed rows at those sizes reach the default budget
and then construct the optimum with Knuth; their unsuccessful search work
is retained. The chain family uses an explicit diagnostic budget of two
million search operations. Its completed and budget-exhausted rows are:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rrrrl}
\toprule
$n$ & Necessary $h$ & Expanded states & Search work & Completion route\\
\midrule
6 & 6 & 67 & 4221 & Lazy\\
8 & 8 & 382 & 32,013 & Lazy\\
10 & 10 & 2037 & 211,825 & Lazy\\
12 & 12 & 10,216 & 1,267,315 & Lazy\\
14 & 14 & 12,587 & 2,000,001 & Knuth fallback\\
16 & 16 & 8689 & 2,000,001 & Knuth fallback\\
18 & 18 & 6554 & 2,000,001 & Knuth fallback\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The record \texttt{slot\_lazy\_scale.json} retains every original input,
height, budget, search and fallback counter, separate reference counter,
seed and eight source hashes. These finite counts are not a fitted exponent
or a speed claim. Uniform trees already have a linear constructor.

A separate unbudgeted audit for Theorem~\ref{thm:lazy-exponential}
completes $n=6,8,10,12$, checking all 14, 62, 254 and 1022 distinct states
in \eqref{eq:lazy-forced-count}. Their canonical paths contain 14, 62,
254 and 1022 distinct edges. Every edge is checked for skyline membership,
feasibility, strict comparison of its candidate bound with the exact
parent optimum, and presence of its child in the completed search cache.
The full runs cache 68, 383, 2038 and 10,217 exact states, including base
states. Post-search bound comparisons add operations to this audit's
counters; the scale table above reports construction search work alone.
The record \texttt{slot\_lazy\_barrier.json} retains all counters and
eight source hashes. Its larger rows, through $n=256$, evaluate the proved
subset-count formula only. The theorem follows from the explicit paths and
strict dominance, independently of these finite checks.

\clearpage
\section{Unimodal domination and certified-envelope audit}
\label{app:unimodal-envelope}
The audit exhausts 920 unimodal words over $\{0,1,2\}$ through eight keys
and checks all 136,802 nonempty subsequences for componentwise domination
by the constructed contiguous block. Its independent definition rejects
8920 nonunimodal words. Another 1589 nonnegative reward-template controls,
from 12,864 literal subsequences, check the optimization identity without
assuming ruler rewards.

On 236 mixed inputs through 32 keys, including monotone, constant, isolated
peak, geometric, irregular unimodal and rational weights, the original
all-root recurrence checks 31,438 interval values and contiguous witnesses,
560 complete above-capacity support sets, and 24,032 densities. All 99,398
eligible small square controls satisfy $2s-1$. The original recurrence
uses 565,705 root candidates and 65,081 states.

The arbitrary-input audit includes every binary word at nine and ten keys,
plus 100 random mixed inputs through 32 keys, totaling 1636 inputs.
It independently checks 101,300 interval-unimodality decisions, 200,892
upper-cost inequalities, 91,143 certified exact interval values, and
130,408 envelope densities. The original recurrence checks 38,221 exact
certified densities and 4988 certified support sets, using 2,345,440 root
candidates and 435,040 states. The two signed-correction examples have eight
literal score controls from 253 subsequences, separate from those recurrence
checks.

Fourteen larger sparse constructions reach 65,536 keys. They have 1168
sampled literal ruler-window controls and 2780 constructed local-square
controls; five single-key rows also match the sharp closed-form count.
They do not have original all-root layer references at those sizes.
At $n=65,536$ both fixtures perform 917,538 ruler-window updates and
983,026 range-tree build-node operations. The isolated-peak input outputs
65,294 core entries across all layers, and the irregular rational mountain
outputs 1201. All entries are constructed without scanning interval pairs.

The record is \texttt{unimodal\_above\_audit.json}. It retains exact operation
counts, both signed-correction witnesses, verification scopes, and the final
executed-source SHA-256 manifest. The support and work bounds follow from
contiguous domination, the ruler recurrence and visibility geometry.

\clearpage
\section{Valley domination, implicit queries, and dense-square audit}
\label{app:valley-audit}
The independent audit exhausts 920 valley words over $\{0,1,2\}$ through
eight keys. It checks all 136,802 nonempty subsequences for componentwise
domination by the selected end blocks, and 6198 arbitrary nonnegative
reward vectors. An independent definition rejects 8920 nonvalley words.

On 256 mixed inputs through 64 keys, the original all-root recurrence
checks 56,076 above-capacity interval values and their two-block witnesses.
Inputs include monotone and constant words, geometric and irregular valleys,
rational weights, and nonzero gap weights. The original recurrence uses
1,396,993 root candidates and 113,799 states. The explicit dense family
and its normalized rational version have 672 square cells checked against
that recurrence, at sides four, eight, and sixteen. Each square also has
the proved exact support count $s^2/2$.

Fourteen larger implicit constructions reach 65,536 keys. Their checks
comprise 896 sampled literal largest-key selections, 896 literal ruler-prefix
sums at arbitrary lengths, and 640 sampled dense-formula cells. These are
separate from the original all-root controls; no original all-root layer is
computed at the large sizes. At $n=65,536$, each construction performs
1,835,076 ruler-window updates and stores 1,966,150 window entries across
the forward and reversed arrays, including their zero bases. No interval
table or dense core is constructed.

The record \texttt{valley\_above\_audit.json} retains operation counts and
the executed-source SHA-256 manifest. The theoretical work bounds follow
from the two-wing binary search and ruler-prefix recurrence; the core
lower bound follows from \eqref{eq:valley-density} and its residue count.

\clearpage
\section{Valley support charges and sparse-core construction audit}
\label{app:valley-core-audit}
The audit uses 1513 small inputs: all 1461 ternary valley words through
nine keys, 48 varied mixed inputs through 32 keys, and four integer or
normalized rational dense-family inputs. Literal sorted sums check 73,082
auxiliary densities, 32,810 two-threshold formulas, and 4267 complete
auxiliary support sets. The same number of constructed ruler support sets
agree with dense evaluation of the independently audited query representation.
Every eligible small square gives a boundary-mass control, totaling 198,321.
Integer inputs additionally check the support count against that mass.

Original all-root mixed layers, separately from those query controls, check
49,278 densities and 2644 complete original support sets on 972 inputs.
They use 1,006,805 root candidates and 163,435 states. The nonvalley word
$p^+$ in Section~\ref{sec:window-envelope-correction} has a separate original
control, with 495 root candidates and 135 states, confirming original
density one and auxiliary density zero.

Twelve larger constructions through 16,384 keys enumerate all above-capacity
cores on binary end blocks and bounded integer valleys. Their 3264 sampled
literal density controls are kept separate from the original all-root
controls. At the largest size the binary input emits 4084 original entries
from 8178 auxiliary events; the bounded valley emits 16,477 from 32,995.
Both preprocessings perform 393,276 ruler-window updates. Five complete
dense-family constructions also check 5456 cells
against \eqref{eq:valley-density}, through side 64. The record
\texttt{valley\_core\_audit.json} retains all operation counts, scopes,
the nonvalley witness, and executed-source hashes. The support and work
bounds follow from the charge and event-enumeration proofs.

\clearpage
\section{Periodic phase construction and full-tree audit}
\label{app:periodic-obst}

The independent audit checks 1042 small inputs: all mixed binary vectors
through four keys, every binary key/gap period through length three at
several complete and incomplete repetition lengths, and seeded geometric,
zero, rational, and final-gap-defect inputs through 48 keys. Literal period
enumeration checks every detected joint period. Independent all-root tables
check 62,376 original interval values and 54,672 leftmost roots. Extended
all-root tables additionally check all 38,756 synthetic phase states used
in the cyclic cancellation, including intervals crossing the input endpoint.
These references use 1,993,994 root candidates and 154,391 states. Separate
ordered-tree enumeration evaluates 7882 shapes on the small exhaustive cases.

Twenty larger matched instances through 512 keys use an independent ordinary
Knuth recurrence, which examines 1,744,227 candidates. Every reconstructed
tree is traversed independently, checking inorder placement, all child links,
all key and gap depths, and weighted cost. Sixteen further complete
constructions reach 262,144 keys, with periods one, three, and five and both
zero and positive gap patterns. At that size the period-five mixed input uses
exactly 2,621,435 root candidates, and the period-three input uses 1,572,861,
as predicted by \eqref{eq:periodic-telescope}. The all-zero case reconstructs
the full leftmost-optimal chain iteratively. These large rows verify complete
construction, exact operation counts, and independently evaluated tree costs;
they do not claim an independent quadratic optimality reference at that size.

The result record retains optimal costs, reconstruction counts,
verification scopes, and executed-source hashes. No timing or fitted exponent
is used. The complexity and optimality claims follow from the exact quotient
\eqref{eq:periodic-recurrence} and cyclic amortization
\eqref{eq:periodic-telescope}, both of which include the synthetic phases.

\clearpage
\section{Boundary extension and full mixed-tree audit}
\label{app:fringe-obst}

The implementation first checks Wilber's LWS primitive against a fresh dense
recurrence: 768 instances through 64 vertices, with signed finite row and
column offsets and quadratic separation costs, use 549,120 literal arc
candidates. All values and leftmost predecessors agree. The implementation
follows the two-search batching algorithm in Wilber's Figure~1. Invalid
cells internal to SMAWK use an ordered tag above all finite values; valid
arc queries remain finite. Each batch's comparisons therefore equal those
of a sufficiently large finite completion. The implementation records
batch widths and checks that their sum is at most three times the number
of vertices; this is the potential argument from Wilber's linear bound.

The full mixed audit checks 980 small inputs: all binary mixed vectors
through four keys and seeded periodic-middle inputs through 27 keys,
including rational weights and boundary weights as large as $10^{40}$.
Explicit middle selection and automatic selection are checked separately.
Independent all-root tables use 351,256 candidates and 49,492 states,
verifying 57,416 original interval values and 44,230 stored root witnesses.
Another 6944 rightmost-root controls test the new-column reduction directly
against the original tables, independently of the periodic solver. Literal
substring enumeration checks 3324 longest-middle decisions using 162,564
candidate substrings. Small ordered-tree enumeration evaluates 7880 shapes.
Every reconstructed tree is independently traversed to check ordering,
links, key and gap depths, and weighted cost.

Sixteen matched inputs through 512 keys use the ordinary uncompressed Knuth
recurrence, examining 1,847,784 candidates. Twelve larger complete automatic
constructions reach 65,537 keys, with periods one, three, and five and one
or seven arbitrary fringe keys. At that size, the one-key right fringe and
period-one middle have exactly 65,537 new boundary states, 131,071 central
root candidates, and 1,309,476 valid LWS transition queries. With period
five and seven fringe keys, the corresponding counts are 458,738, 655,295,
and 8,264,895. The automatic scan performs 1,113,976 signature comparisons
on each of these largest inputs. These are operation counts; central root
candidates and LWS transition queries are different operations.

The large rows verify complete construction and independently evaluated tree
costs; they have no separate quadratic optimality oracle. The work bound
comes from Lemma~\ref{lem:fringe-extension} and the exact state identity
\eqref{eq:fringe-states}. The result record retains the seed, costs, operation
counts, verification scopes, and six executed-source hashes. No timing or
fitted exponent enters the theorem.

\clearpage
\section{Affine translation and finite-domain construction audit}
\label{app:affine-period}

Before running the quotient solver, the harness checks the shape-cost
identity on 6250 literal ordered trees through seven keys. The transformations
include positive scales below and above one and offsets of both signs.
The full audit then checks 1063 small mixed inputs: all binary key/gap
vectors through four keys, seeded affine inputs through 32 keys, and 21
finite inputs whose hypothetical continuations develop negative weights.
Explicit witnesses include periods close to the input length, testing the
terminal diagonals as well as the complete phase cycles.

Independent all-root tables examine 640,661 candidates and 74,877 states.
They check 84,584 original interval values, all 74,877 leftmost roots, and
28,534 stored phase states. Ordered-tree enumeration separately checks
7882 small optimal costs. Recognition has an independent reference: solving
the original two-variable linear equations checks 8644 period-feasibility
decisions, and 860 automatic inputs verify the smallest admissible period.
This reference does not use the production adjacent-sum test. Another
2916 checks validate returned parameter witnesses directly.

Sixteen matched inputs through 512 keys use the ordinary uncompressed
Knuth recurrence, examining 1,393,110 candidates. Nine larger arithmetic-drift
constructions reach 65,537 keys, with periods one, three, and five. At that
size the period-one construction examines exactly 131,073 root candidates;
the period-five case examines 655,325 phase candidates and 18 terminal
candidates. The candidate bounds and exact state count are checked against
\eqref{eq:affine-tail-candidates} and \eqref{eq:affine-states}.
Two further exact geometric constructions reach 4095 keys; their integers
grow with the input length, so their counts concern arithmetic work.

Every output is independently traversed to check key order, child links,
key and gap depths, and weighted cost. The eleven largest construction rows
have no separate quadratic optimality reference; their optimality guarantee
is Theorem~\ref{thm:affine-period}. The result record retains the seed,
operation counts, exact costs, per-row reference scopes, and four source
hashes. No timing, fitted exponent, or infinite positive extension enters
the proof.

\section*{\textsuperscript{*}Dedication}
Glory first be to The Holy One, Blessed be He (\hebrewname), from whom all good things come.  To Him we attribute the ability that has provided for all good to come and be demonstrated anywhere in our work,
and declare ourselves unworthy servants. If in any way we have erred, or our work is unsatisfactory,
we ask mercy and kindness, for our effort is good and earnest.
\end{document}
